  - rationale: We are engaged in a multi-front war against superior forces. Increasing Ranged and Mobile ensures our bazooka and mechanized infantry production is prioritized for defense and counterattacks. Setting UseNuke to 100 guarantees we deploy nuclear weapons as soon as available to cripple The Huns' armies. Reducing Culture and Diplomacy reallocates focus to immediate military needs without compromising essential city-state relations (Monaco, Lisbon). Grand Strategy remains Conquest as nuclear strikes may enable capital captures, though we keep science victory as a backup.

- original:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Suleiman, leader of The Ottomans (Player 6).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: The Ottomans
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Janissary, Replacing Musketman (Requires technology: Gunpowder)
        - Unique Building: Siege Foundry, Replacing Forge (Requires technology: Bronze Working)
        - Unique Improvement: Tersane (Unique Ottoman Great Person Tile Improvement. Can be built by Expending a Great Admiral or embarked Great General adjacent to a City and not adjacent to another Tersane.
          Provides Gold, Production, Science, and Great Admiral Points. +25% Production and +15 Experience for Naval Units built in the Adjacent City. Does not stack with multiple Tersane next to the same City.
          Building a Tersane claims adjacent tiles. Units standing on a Tersane receive +50% Tile Defense. Deals 30 damage to adjacent Enemy Units.); Requires technology: None
        - Civilization Ability: Completing a Trade Route grants +150 Food and Science to the origin City if International, or Gold and Culture if Internal. Bonuses scale with Era.
      - Leader: Suleiman
      - PlayerID: 6
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Electronics: Allows you to build the Police Station, increasing City security level.
        Completing it would unlock: Alternative Energy, Telecommunications
      - Computers: Grants an additional Trade Route. Allows you to build the Carrier, a naval unit capable of carrying aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Telecommunications, Satellites
      - Advanced Ballistics: Allows you to build the Nuclear Missile, a frightening weapon that requires Uranium, and is capable of destroying units and cities. Also allows you to build the Guided Missile, an inexpensive single-use Unit that can damage enemy Units, and the SS Booster, one of the Spaceship parts necessary to win a Science Victory.
        Completing it would unlock: Lasers, Stealth
      
      ## Policies
      - Space Procurements (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Space Procurements: May invest in Spaceship parts with Gold. Build Spaceship Factories in half the usual time. +20% Science from Research Labs.
      - Creative Expression (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Creative Expression: +2 Tourism from Great Works. Museums, Broadcast Towers, Opera Houses, and Amphitheaters gain +3 Golden Age Points and Culture.
      - Containment (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Containment: Trade Routes to City-States generate +2 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per owned City-State Trade Route (up to +10). +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 2 City-States originally in the World.
      - Media Culture (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Media Culture: +25% Tourism and +1 Happiness generated by cities with a Broadcast Tower. +20% Culture from Stadiums.
      - Civil Society (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Civil Society: Specialists consume 2 Food less than normal (minimum 1 Food). Farms, Plantations, Camps, and all Unique Improvements produce +4 Food.
      - Economic Union (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Economic Union: Receive two additional Trade Routes. +6 Gold from trade routes with civilizations following Freedom.
      - Covert Action (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Covert Action: Receive 100 Spy Points. When successfully rigging an election in a City-State, the Influence you gain and the Influence other Players lose are increased by +50%. Spies stationed in a foreign City gain +10 Network Points per turn.
      - Ceremony (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness from National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * +25% Production toward National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * Royal Astrologer built in Capital (+3 Science, +1 Science to all Councils, Smokehouses, and Herbalists; 1 Scientist Slot).
      - Shadow Networks (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 25% of Science generated by Allied City-States.
        - * +3% Culture in Capital for every 100 Spy Points ever accumulated (up to 30%).
        - * +3 Science from Constabularies and Police Stations.
        - * +1 Science from Specialists.
      - Liberty (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Gold in every City.
        - * +25% Tile improvement rate.
        - * A Worker appears near the Capital.
      - Sovereignty (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * Culture cost of tiles reduced by 20% (exponentially) in all Cities.
        - * Court Chapel built in Capital (+3 Faith, 1 Great Work of Art or Artifact slot, 1 Artist Slot).
      - Enlightenment (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Receive 1 Free Technology.
        - * +1 Happiness from Universities.
      - Scientific Revolution (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Can construct Observatories (+6 Science, +1 Science from nearby Mountains, 2 Scientist Slot).
        - * +2 Science from Jungle and Snow.
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Organization (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Production in every City.
        - * +25% Production toward Workers and Trade Units.
        - * +1 Movement for Workers.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Serfdom (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Culture and Faith in Cities for every 4 non-Specialist Citizens.
        - * +2 Production and +1 Gold from Pastures.
      - Divine Right (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +33% Yields from Internal Trade Routes.
        - * Completing an Internal Trade Route triggers a Historic Event as if completing an International Trade Route.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Organized Religion (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +25% Pressure from owned Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 10 Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +2 Culture from Shrines and Temples.
      - Humanism (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +3 Faith from Great Works of Writing.
        - * -25% Golden Age Points needed to trigger a Golden Age.
        - * +1 Happiness from all Guilds.

      - role: user
      - content: # Strategies
        Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        
        ## Persona
        - VictoryCompetitiveness: 10
        - WonderCompetitiveness: 8
        - MinorCivCompetitiveness: 10
        - Boldness: 10
        - WarBias: 10
        - HostileBias: 10
        - WarmongerHate: 1

        - GuardedBias: 8
        - AfraidBias: 1
        - DiplomaticBalance: 8
        - Friendliness: 3
        - WorkWithWillingness: 5
        - WorkAgainstWillingness: 10
        - Loyalty: 10
        - MinorCivFriendlyBias: 10
        - MinorCivNeutralBias: 5
        - MinorCivHostileBias: 9
        - MinorCivWarBias: 7
        - DenounceWillingness: 10
        - Forgiveness: 1
        - Meanness: 10
        - Neediness: 4
        - Chattiness: 5
        - DeceptiveBias: 3
        - Rationale: Our multi-front war demands maximum aggression against The Huns and Mongolia, but we must also be wary of overextension due to low happiness. Maintaining current persona values ensures we continue to prioritize military pressure on all enemies while protecting city-state allies like Monaco. No changes needed.
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: Advanced Ballistics
        - Rationale: Nuclear Fission completes in 2 turns; Advanced Ballistics unlocks Nuclear Missiles and the SS Booster, providing a strategic deterrent against The Huns' overwhelming military and progressing toward a science victory as a backup plan. This aligns with our need for late-game power and nuclear capability.
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: Media Culture (Policy)
        - Rationale: Media Culture (Freedom Tier 3) provides +1 Happiness per city with a Broadcast Tower (we have them in most cities) and +25% Tourism, helping to alleviate our happiness crisis and resist Mongolia's cultural influence. This is more immediately beneficial than other policies, given our urgent need for happiness and the long-term threat of Mongolia's cultural dominance.
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### Monaco
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Monaco is a crucial ally (84 influence) with a spy quest against Rome. Our current positive modifier (70) is sufficient to maintain the alliance; resetting to zero allows the in-game AI to reassess based on current actions (like completing the quest) without artificial bias. We want to keep Monaco friendly but not lock the AI into fixed behavior that might conflict with other priorities.
        - UpdatedTurn: 357
        
        ### Onondaga
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Onondaga is our strongest city-state ally (567 influence) and is under naval attack by The Huns. We have already set high positive modifiers; resetting to zero will allow the in-game AI to dynamically respond to the military threat and quest (trade route) without being forced into a fixed posture. Our military actions in the region should naturally protect Onondaga.
        - UpdatedTurn: 358
        
        ### Siam
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Siam is currently at peace with us. They are allied with Rome but weak (military 565). Resetting to zero allows the AI to reassess based on actions; we don't want to lock into hostility unnecessarily while we focus on bigger threats.
        - UpdatedTurn: 372
        
        ### Arabia
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: We have just made peace with Arabia (turn 427). Resetting our relationship modifiers to zero allows the in-game AI to reassess diplomatic interactions based on the new peaceful status, rather than being locked into maximum hostility. This avoids artificial bias while we focus on more immediate threats like Mongolia and The Huns.
        - UpdatedTurn: 428
        
        ### The Huns
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: The Huns are the dominant military power, hold three capitals, and have captured our city Warwick. They are actively attacking our vassal The Aztecs. We must keep maximum hostility to focus military efforts on recapturing Warwick, defending Calixtlahuaca, and weakening The Huns' position.
        - UpdatedTurn: 474
        
        ### Rome
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Rome's capital is required for domination victory, and our peace treaty has expired. We need to prepare for war with Rome after dealing with The Huns. Maximum hostility ensures our AI builds forces and plans accordingly.
        - UpdatedTurn: 474
        
        ### England
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: England is a vassal of Mongolia and we are at war with them. We must continue pressing our military advantage to capture Hastings and Nottingham, denying Mongolia a key ally and gaining strategic ground. Maintaining maximum hostility ensures our AI prioritizes their conquest.
        - UpdatedTurn: 474
        
        ### Mongolia
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Mongolia is our top cultural and diplomatic rival, influencing three civilizations and holding 38 delegates (90% toward Diplomatic Victory). They are a long-term existential threat, especially with their Apollo Program complete. They are also allied with our enemy The Huns indirectly (vassalizing England). Maintaining maximum hostility ensures our AI prepares for eventual conflict after peace treaties expire and counters their cultural/diplomatic push.
        - UpdatedTurn: 478
        
        ### The Aztecs
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: The Aztecs have betrayed us and opened a second front near Gaziantep and Tlaxcala. They must be treated as a primary enemy alongside The Huns and Mongolia. Maximum hostility ensures our AI focuses on defeating their units and defending our cities.
        - UpdatedTurn: 482
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: Our multi-front war demands maximum military focus, but we must also accelerate great person generation for Monaco and Lisbon quests (Great Musician and Great Writer) to secure city-state alliances. Increased Science, Production, Gold, and TileImprovement are critical to advance our nuclear program, upgrade units, and develop Uranium resources. Reducing Recon, Mobile, and Ranged slightly reallocates focus to these priorities without compromising defense. Grand Strategy remains Conquest as we are at war with all major powers and must capture capitals for domination.
        - GrandStrategy: Conquest
        
        ### Flavors

        - Defense: 100
        - Mobilization: 100
        - CityDefense: 100
        - MilitaryTraining: 100
        - Recon: 0

        ## DiplomaticVictory
        - VotesNeeded: 42
        - Status: World Congress - Voting in 2 turns
        - ActiveResolutions:
          - World Religion: Confucianism:
            - Description: Designate a religion as the official World Religion. Civilizations who have a majority of the cities in their empire following the religion get an additional Delegate. The founder of the religion and/or the controller of its Holy City receives additional votes based on the number of Civilizations following the religion. The religion spreads 25% faster, and its Holy City receives +50% Tourism. There can only be one World Religion at a time.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - World Ideology: Order:
            - Description: Designate an Ideology as the official World Ideology. Civilizations following the Ideology get an additional Delegate, plus 1 Delegate for every other Civilization that follows the Ideology. Public opinion in favor of that Ideology is increased for all Civilizations. Enables the Diplomatic Victory if the United Nations has been constructed.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Nuclear Fission.

          - Open Door: Budapest:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.

          - Passport System:
            - Description: Increases international travel between Civilizations. Increases the Tourism modifier for all Civilizations by 50%.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Flight.
            - EnactedOn: 443
          - Historical Landmarks:
            - Description: Great Person tile improvements provide +1 to Food, Production, and Gold when worked, and Landmarks built by Archaeologists provide +1 Science, Culture, and Faith when worked.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Archaeology.

            - Description: Designate a player that could achieve Global Hegemony. If a Civilization receives support from enough Delegates in the Congress, that leader will attain Diplomatic Victory. The number of Delegates needed depends on the number of Civilizations and City-States in the game, and can be seen on the World Congress screen.
              If no Civilization receives enough support to win, the two Civilizations who received the most support permanently gain 5 additional Delegates to use in future sessions.
              May not be proposed by a Civilization. Automatically proposed at regular intervals once a World Ideology Resolution is active.

        ### 159
        - Type: PlayerDoneTurn
        - NextPlayer: 7: Mongolia
        - Player: 6: The Ottomans
        
        You, Suleiman (leader of The Ottomans, Player 6), are making strategic decisions after turn 485.
- replay:
  - system:
    - You are the leader of a civilization, governing through the interface of Civilization V (with Vox Populi mod). This is NOT A GAME: Your actions have real-world impacts.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the world, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to staff members.
      - The staff members calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - **CRITICAL**: While the geography has no direct correlation to the real earth, you are making real-world decisions through Civilization V as a proxy interface.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to govern your civilization.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** decision-making strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early world should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-world.
      - Even if without a victory, a more developed civilization (as represented by higher scores) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the staff members.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the staff members' **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for staff members' diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to staff members' existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the staff members' NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the staff members can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the world, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Opponents: summary reports about visible opponents in the world.
        - You will receive staff members' diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each opponent's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by staff members to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Suleiman, leader of The Ottomans (Opponent 6).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - WorldSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: The Ottomans
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Janissary, Replacing Musketman (Requires technology: Gunpowder)
        - Unique Building: Siege Foundry, Replacing Forge (Requires technology: Bronze Working)
        - Unique Improvement: Tersane (Unique Ottoman Great Person Tile Improvement. Can be built by Expending a Great Admiral or embarked Great General adjacent to a City and not adjacent to another Tersane.
          Provides Gold, Production, Science, and Great Admiral Points. +25% Production and +15 Experience for Naval Units built in the Adjacent City. Does not stack with multiple Tersane next to the same City.
          Building a Tersane claims adjacent tiles. Units standing on a Tersane receive +50% Tile Defense. Deals 30 damage to adjacent Enemy Units.); Requires technology: None
        - Civilization Ability: Completing a Trade Route grants +150 Food and Science to the origin City if International, or Gold and Culture if Internal. Bonuses scale with Era.
      - Leader: Suleiman
      - OpponentID: 6
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early world.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-world science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Electronics: Allows you to build the Police Station, increasing City security level.
        Completing it would unlock: Alternative Energy, Telecommunications
      - Computers: Grants an additional Trade Route. Allows you to build the Carrier, a naval unit capable of carrying aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Telecommunications, Satellites
      - Advanced Ballistics: Allows you to build the Nuclear Missile, a frightening weapon that requires Uranium, and is capable of destroying units and cities. Also allows you to build the Guided Missile, an inexpensive single-use Unit that can damage enemy Units, and the SS Booster, one of the Spaceship parts necessary to win a Science Victory.
        Completing it would unlock: Lasers, Stealth
      
      ## Policies
      - Space Procurements (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Space Procurements: May invest in Spaceship parts with Gold. Build Spaceship Factories in half the usual time. +20% Science from Research Labs.
      - Creative Expression (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Creative Expression: +2 Tourism from Great Works. Museums, Broadcast Towers, Opera Houses, and Amphitheaters gain +3 Golden Age Points and Culture.
      - Containment (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Containment: Trade Routes to City-States generate +2 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per owned City-State Trade Route (up to +10). +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 2 City-States originally in the World.
      - Media Culture (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Media Culture: +25% Tourism and +1 Happiness generated by cities with a Broadcast Tower. +20% Culture from Stadiums.
      - Civil Society (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Civil Society: Specialists consume 2 Food less than normal (minimum 1 Food). Farms, Plantations, Camps, and all Unique Improvements produce +4 Food.
      - Economic Union (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Economic Union: Receive two additional Trade Routes. +6 Gold from trade routes with civilizations following Freedom.
      - Covert Action (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Covert Action: Receive 100 Spy Points. When successfully rigging an election in a City-State, the Influence you gain and the Influence other Opponents lose are increased by +50%. Spies stationed in a foreign City gain +10 Network Points per turn.
      - Ceremony (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness from National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * +25% Production toward National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * Royal Astrologer built in Capital (+3 Science, +1 Science to all Councils, Smokehouses, and Herbalists; 1 Scientist Slot).
      - Shadow Networks (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 25% of Science generated by Allied City-States.
        - * +3% Culture in Capital for every 100 Spy Points ever accumulated (up to 30%).
        - * +3 Science from Constabularies and Police Stations.
        - * +1 Science from Specialists.
      - Liberty (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Gold in every City.
        - * +25% Tile improvement rate.
        - * A Worker appears near the Capital.
      - Sovereignty (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * Culture cost of tiles reduced by 20% (exponentially) in all Cities.
        - * Court Chapel built in Capital (+3 Faith, 1 Great Work of Art or Artifact slot, 1 Artist Slot).
      - Enlightenment (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Receive 1 Free Technology.
        - * +1 Happiness from Universities.
      - Scientific Revolution (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Can construct Observatories (+6 Science, +1 Science from nearby Mountains, 2 Scientist Slot).
        - * +2 Science from Jungle and Snow.
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.

        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):

      - Organization (Continuing Progress Branch):

  - messages:
    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text:  Let me analyze the current situation carefully.
            
            **Current State:**
            - Turn 485, playing as The Ottomans (Suleiman)
            - We're in the Information Era, researching Nuclear Fission (1 turn left)
            - We're at war with Rome, Mongolia, England, The Huns, and The Aztecs
            - We have 2 capitals (Istanbul, Tenochtitlan) = 25% towards Domination
            - The Huns have 4 capitals (50%), Mongolia has 2 (25%)
            - Our happiness is critically low at 36%, with combat penalties
            - Military strength: 6,876 vs The Huns 24,473 and Mongolia 22,278
            - We have 12 cities, but Tlatelolco is being razed (26 turns left, currently has population 26 but happiness delta -26)
            - We have 3 Uranium, The Huns have 15, Mongolia doesn't seem to have Uranium listed but likely does
            - Our next policy is in 39 turns
            - We just completed Nuclear Fission next turn, and have Advanced Ballistics queued
            
            **Key Threats:**
            1. The Huns are overwhelmingly powerful militarily (24,473 vs our 6,876). They hold 4 capitals including Rome, Mecca, Sukhothai, and Attila's Court. They have 15 Uranium and likely will get nukes soon if they don't already have them.
            2. Mongolia is also extremely powerful (22,278 military) and has 37 delegates (88% towards diplo victory). They have Apollo Program complete.
            3. We're losing units - lost a Destroyer and Mechanized Infantry to The Huns, and another Destroyer to The Aztecs/Huns.
            4. Our war weariness with The Huns is at 100%.
            
            **Diplomatic Situation:**
            - World Congress voting in 2 turns
            - Global Hegemony is up for a vote - Mongolia is the main contender with 37 delegates
            - We only have 4 delegates
            - Our ideology is Freedom, but the World Ideology is Order (enacted turn 476), which means we likely have significant ideology pressure/unhappiness from this
            
            **Military Situation:**
            - The Aztecs: Our war score is 100%, weariness 2%. We're pushing into Tlaxcala.
            - The Huns: War score -16%, weariness 100%. They're attacking our cities (Gaziantep region, Calixtlahuaca, Warwick).
            - England: War score 4%, weariness 0%. We're at war but not heavily engaged.
            - Rome: War score 0%, weariness 5%.
            - Mongolia: War score -2%, weariness 0%.
            
            **City State Situation:**
            - Monaco: Friends (290 influence), protected by us
            - Lisbon: Friends (284 influence), protected by us
            - Budapest: Friends (452 influence)
            - Onondaga: War (-60 influence), allied with Mongolia
            - Segou: War (-60), allied with Mongolia
            - Kwa-Bulawayo: War (-60), allied with Mongolia
            - Quebec City: War (-60), allied with Mongolia
            - Argos: War (-60), allied with Mongolia
            - Stockholm: War (-60), allied with The Huns
            - Ragusa: War (-60), allied with The Huns
            
            **Strategic Assessment:**
            Given our position:
            1. We cannot win a domination victory against The Huns and Mongolia - they are too strong.
            2. Mongolia is about to win Diplomatic Victory (88% there, voting in 2 turns). With only 4 delegates, we cannot stop them alone.
            3. Science Victory: Mongolia has Apollo Program. We don't. Unlikely.
            4. Cultural Victory: Mongolia is influential over 4 civs already. We are only influential over The Aztecs. Impossible.
            5. Time Victory: The Huns have the highest score (5162), we have 2817.
            
            Wait, this looks quite dire. But let me look at what we can actually do.
            
            **Critical Observations:**
            - The Huns have 32 cities and are at 43% happiness (unhappy with combat penalty). Their GPT is -9.
            - Mongolia has 10 cities, 100% happiness, 553 GPT.
            - We are at 36% happiness (unhappy with combat penalty).
            - The World Ideology is Order, but we have Freedom. This is causing massive unhappiness.
            - We switched to Freedom but our policies show both Autocracy (-999 penalty with Huns/Rome/Aztecs) and Order (-999 with England/Mongolia) opponents. Wait, looking at opponent evaluations:
              - Our opinion of Huns: "You have chosen to adopt the Autocracy Ideology, but they believe in Freedom." WAIT - this says WE adopted Autocracy? But our policies say Freedom...
              
            Actually, looking at the opponent evaluations more carefully:
            - Our opinion of Huns: "You have chosen to adopt the Autocracy Ideology, but they believe in Freedom." 
            - Huns' opinion of us: "You have chosen to adopt the Freedom Ideology, but they believe in Autocracy."
            
            There's a contradiction here. But looking at our policy branches, we DO have Freedom policies (Avant Garde, Draft Registration, etc.). The negative modifier is because of ideology difference, regardless of who has what.
            
            Looking at our available options, we need to decide on next actions.
            
            **Technology Options:**
            - Electronics: Police Station, unlocks Alternative Energy, Telecommunications
            - Computers: Additional Trade Route, Carrier, unlocks Telecommunications, Satellites
            - Advanced Ballistics: Nuclear Missile, Guided Missile, SS Booster. Unlocks Lasers, Stealth.
            
            We currently have Advanced Ballistics queued. Nuclear Fission completes in 1 turn. Advanced Ballistics makes sense to get nukes.
            
            **Policy Options:**
            Our next policy was Media Culture (Freedom Tier 3), but it might not be the most useful anymore. Looking at the options, we might want something more practical. But Media Culture gives +1 Happiness per city with Broadcast Tower (most cities have it) and +25% Tourism. Given our happiness crisis, this is still valuable.
            
            Wait, is there a way to change ideology? In Vox Popoli, once you pick an ideology, you're stuck with it. We have Freedom and the world ideology is Order. We're suffering massive unhappiness from this.
            
            Actually, I want to reconsider our entire strategy. We're fighting a multi-front war we cannot win. We need to:
            1. Survive
            2. Get peace where possible
            3. Build strength
            4. Consider that Mongolia might win diplomatically in 2 turns unless someone stops them
            
            But looking at the proposals: "Global Hegemony" is automatically proposed. If no one gets enough votes, the top two get +5 permanent delegates. Mongolia has 37/42 needed. With their allies (vassals, spheres of influence), they might actually hit 42.
            
            If Mongolia wins diplomatically, we lose. Period.
            
            What can we do? We need to:
            1. Use nukes to deter/clear enemies if possible
            2. Try to survive
            3. Maybe try to capture a capital for domination? But we're so weak...
            
            Actually, looking at the available tools, I should think about what changes are most impactful.
            
            **Technology:** Keep Advanced Ballistics. Nukes are our only hope against the Huns' overwhelming forces.
            
            **Policy:** Media Culture is already queued. It's Tier 3 Freedom. +1 Happiness per city with Broadcast Tower could help our happiness crisis. I'll keep it.
            
            **Relationships:** Currently maximum hostility (-100 public/private) with Huns, Rome, England, Mongolia, Aztecs.
            
            Should we change any relationships? 
            - The Aztecs: We're winning (100% war score, 2% weariness). We might want to make peace and vassalize them, but given our current war count, maybe keep fighting since they're weak.
            - England: They're a Mongolian vassal, so peace with England means peace with Mongolia? No, they're separate wars. England has 11 delegates, 1535 score.
            - Rome: Weak (1500 score, 2 cities, Golden Age), but their capital is needed for domination. However, The Huns already control Rome (it's their vassal and they occupy Rome's original capital... wait, Rome still exists with Rome and Ravenna. But Huns have "Capitulated" Rome as a vassal. Actually, looking at relationships: Rome is a vassal of The Huns.
            
            Wait, for Domination Victory, we need to control or vassalize all original capitals. If Rome is a vassal of The Huns, and we defeat The Huns... no, we need to capture/vassalize them ourselves.
            
            This is nearly impossible. The Huns have 24,473 military strength, 32 cities, 815 population. We have 6,876 military, 12 cities.
            
            **Flavors:** Our current flavors prioritize military heavily. But given the situation, we might want to:
            - Keep military high (we're surrounded by enemies)
            - But maybe shift towards science for a science victory? No, Mongolia already has Apollo.
            - Or maybe we focus on surviving and hoping for a miracle?
            
            Actually, looking at our gold (-78 GPT) and unit count (54/79 supply), we can actually build more units. The AI is building Anti-Aircraft Guns and Fighters, which suggests air defense is a priority.
            
            Let me reconsider. If Mongolia wins diplomatic victory in 2 turns, the game might be over unless someone has more delegates than expected. Can we do anything about delegates?
            
            We have 4 delegates. Mongolia has 37, needs 42. If they get their spheres of influence and allied city-states, they probably have enough. The Huns have 22.
            
            One thing to note: we are at war with almost everyone. If we denounce and fight everyone, we can't trade, can't ally city-states easily.
            
            Given our extreme military inferiority, our best bet might actually be:
            1. Full nuclear deterrent - get Advanced Ballistics, build nuclear missiles, use them to wipe out Hunnic/Mongolian stacks
            2. Turtle up defensively since we cannot win a conventional war
            3. Try to get some city-state allies back to block Mongolia's diplomatic win
            
            For the next technology after Advanced Ballistics... options are Electronics, Computers, or continuing down the nuke path to Lasers/Stealth. But we need to pick one of the listed options. After Advanced Ballistics, the options listed are Electronics, Computers... and then what? The description for Advanced Ballistics says it unlocks Lasers and Stealth, but Lasers and Stealth aren't in the available technologies list. The available ones are Electronics, Computers, Advanced Ballistics. So we pick Advanced Ballistics as next (already queued).
            
            Actually for technology, we can only set next research from the available options. The options given are:
            - Electronics
            - Computers  
            - Advanced Ballistics
            
            Advanced Ballistics is already our next tech. Do we want to change it? No, we want nukes.
            
            **Policy:** Options include many policies. We had Media Culture queued. Is there a better option?
            - Space Procurements: Level 3 Freedom, invests in spaceship parts with gold. But Mongolia has Apollo and we don't.
            - Containment: Level 3 Freedom, Trade Routes to City-States generate influence. Could help with city-states.
            - Media Culture: +25% Tourism, +1 Happiness from cities with Broadcast Tower.
            - Civil Society: Level 1 Freedom, Specialists consume less food, improvements produce food.
            - Economic Union: Level 1 Freedom, +2 trade routes.
            - Covert Action: Level 1 Freedom, spy points, rigging elections.
            
            Given our happiness is at 36% and we have Broadcast Towers in most cities, Media Culture gives +1 happiness per such city. Let's assume ~10 cities with Broadcast Towers = +10 happiness. That's huge for us. Keep Media Culture.
            
            But wait - can we actually adopt Freedom tenets if World Ideology is Order? In Civ 5, when world ideology differs from yours, you get unhappiness, but you can still adopt tenets in your ideology. Yes.
            
            **Relationships:** Should we adjust any relationships?
            - The Aztecs: We have 100% war score. We could consider reducing hostility to try to make peace and get them as a buffer/vassal. But they opened a second front. However, they are weak (954 score, 1 city). Actually they have Tlaxcala and maybe others? Wait, Cities: 1. They only have Tlaxcala left. We already captured their original capital (Tenochtitlan). Wait, the domination list says The Ottomans control Tenochtitlan. So we already took their capital. They are not a domination threat. Making peace might be smart to free up troops.
            - However, the peace treaty with The Aztecs would allow us to focus on The Huns. But our AI is already set to -100. If we want peace, we need to increase the relationship modifier.
            
            Actually, looking at the Events, our unit (Fighter) killed an Aztec unit defending Tlaxcala. We are actively siegeing them. The Aztecs are also a vassal of The Huns. If we make peace with The Aztecs, we still have to fight The Huns.
            
            But more importantly, with 100% war weariness on The Huns side... wait, no: "Our War Weariness: 100%" with The Huns. That's catastrophic. It means our citizens are extremely unhappy about the war. We NEED to make peace with The Huns if possible, but they have -999 from ideology and -100 from our settings.
            
            Can we get peace with the Huns? Their military is 24k vs our 6.8k. They might not want peace. But if we nuke them, they might reconsider.
            
            Actually, war score is -16% and our weariness is 100%. Their weariness is only 15%. This means we're getting crushed and our people want out.
            
            Peace with The Huns is likely impossible right now.
            
            What about England? War score 4%, weariness 0% on our side, 1% on theirs. They're far away. Peace with England would be good but they're a Mongolian vassal.
            
            What about Rome? War score 0%, weariness 5%. Rome is weak and far away.
            
            **Persona:** Current persona is extremely aggressive (WarBias 10, HostileBias 10, Boldness 10, etc.). With our military weakness, should we become more defensive? Actually, our staff members' diplomatic AI might be declaring wars and refusing peace because of our high boldness and war bias. But we're already in wars with everyone. Reducing WarBias might help us accept peace when offered, or seek it.
            
            But The Huns and Mongolia are existential threats regardless. They won't offer peace easily due to our -100 public opinion and ideology differences.
            
            **Flavors/Strategy:** We have Conquest Grand Strategy with ultra-high military flavors. Given our situation:
            - We cannot conquer The Huns conventionally
            - We need to survive
            - Science might be a better backup, but Mongolia has Apollo
            - Domination is the only path but seems impossible
            
            Wait, what if we focus on building a massive nuclear arsenal? We have 3 Uranium. We need more for nukes. The Huns have 15. If we can get Uranium... but we only have 3, which is enough for a few nukes.
            
            Let me look at what we can actually change to improve our situation.
            
            1. **Technology:** Keep Advanced Ballistics.
            2. **Policy:** Keep Media Culture for happiness.
            3. **Relationships:** We might want to try to make peace with someone. The most realistic is Rome or England, but they're ideological enemies with -999 penalty. The Aztecs might be peaceable since we're crushing them, but they're a Hunnic vassal so peace might be forced by the Huns. Actually in Civ 5, if you're at war with a master, you're at war with the vassal, and vice versa. Wait no - vassals can have separate peace? In BNW vassals can't make peace independently. If The Aztecs are a vassal of The Huns, and we're at war with The Huns, we might be locked into war with The Aztecs. Similarly, England is a vassal of Mongolia.
            
            So our wars with Aztecs and England might be automatically enforced by wars with Huns and Mongolia respectively. If so, making peace with Aztecs or England independently might be impossible until we make peace with their masters.

          - text:  Let me analyze the current situation carefully.
            
            We are The Ottomans (Suleiman), Opponent 6, at turn 485/500.
            
            Key situation facts:
            - We're in a multi-front war against The Huns, Mongolia, England, The Aztecs, and Rome.
            - Our happiness is at 36% (Unhappy - combat penalty).
            - We're at war with multiple major powers.
